# **ChainTorrent MVP**

## Problem Statement

JavaScript dependency distribution runs through a single centralized registry that is an availability chokepoint, a mutable source of truth, and an intermediary between the people who publish packages and the people who consume them. ChainTorrent's MVP replaces that path with a content-addressed swarm, a canonical on-chain identity registry, and transferable access entitlements, so that the distribution and identity model is proven with monetization out of bounds.

**Adoption runs individual developer first, and everything else follows from that.** A developer at a terminal gains cross-project reuse of whatever their machine has already fetched, swarm retrieval on popular packages, and installs that survive a registry outage. Build platforms adopt next and for their own reasons: a service running the same few thousand popular installs continuously, on always-on machines with real bandwidth, holds the ideal cache as a by-product of its own economics, and seeding that cache costs almost nothing once it exists. That makes them natural superseeders rather than participants anyone has to recruit, and it is why no privileged bootstrap host is part of this plan. CI and enterprise adoption arrive as a consequence of that sequence rather than as targets to be won early.

The sequence matters to this workplan because it determines what the MVP measures. Latency is judged against interactive local installs rather than pipeline tolerance, since a developer notices a doubled install where a pipeline does not. Cache reuse is the headline adoption metric, because it is the benefit the adopting population actually experiences. Registry-outage survival remains true throughout and is simply not the lead, since it matters most to the population that adopts last.

The design is specified in `docs/research/cryptography.md` and bounded in `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`. Undetermined decisions are held in the To-Do list below.

The cryptographic construction is specified; its research record is `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`, and its statement is `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`. A validation harness on both pairing curves produces the sizes, timings, and gas against the configured piece-group size and curve defaults, and nothing waits on it: every node reads the piece-group size from the deployment record and depends only on the code it calls.

## Objectives

Deliver an install path for JavaScript dependencies that survives registry outages, serves popular packages from a peer swarm, and exercises the identity and entitlement pipeline end to end.

Prove distribution and identity. Willingness to pay and seeder compensation are out of bounds and are held in the To-Do list; the transaction flow is proven at a nominal price on assets the project publishes, as a test instrument rather than a monetization step.

Every undetermined decision is carried in the To-Do list, which every workplan that stems from this one inherits.

## Expected Outcome

A developer installs dependencies and receives a working `node_modules` assembled from a local content-addressed store, a peer swarm, or the ingest source as fallback, with canonical identity resolved on-chain and an access entitlement minted to their wallet.

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/run/generate_consumer` **The run's consumer of the generate family's factory: `GenerateConsumer`, an `IGenerateConsumer<F>` generic over the delivery proof's forms, constructed from the typed mint and transfer contexts, the admitted mapping identities, and the admitted proof identity, which on receiving the concrete the factory admitted calls its `emit` with those values and returns the concrete's `EmitReturn` whole; names no concrete, calls no factory, and admits nothing; owns the consumer, its constructor params, and its return alias; adds the module to the `run` module**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the harness writes the generated directory through whichever generate concrete `create_generate` constructs from the configured selection, and the concrete's type carries the borrowed adapters' types, so the factory hands it to a consumer rather than returning it; the run therefore holds a consumer that makes the emission, so that `harness-crypto/run/delivery_proof_consumer` passes the consumer to `create_generate` and no run module names a generate concrete, reads a configured value, or admits a context or an identity (the dependency map's `harness-crypto/run/generate_consumer` row, its Dependencies edges, and its conflict flags on the harness run alone calling the factories and on the run being a composing function over consumers it owns; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `harness-crypto/run/generate_consumer`, calling `emit` with the typed contexts and the admitted identities; `harness-crypto/generate/factory`'s `construction`, which names this node as the emission, one `IGenerateConsumer<F>` generic over the forms in its own module)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the consumer is constructed once from a mint context typed `DeliveryContext<F, MintPurpose>`, a transfer context typed `DeliveryContext<F, TransferPurpose>`, the admitted identities the identity mapping covers, and the admitted identity the proofs are made for, each handed typed and admitted, and holds them for every emission it makes
    * `[✅]`   Functional: handed a concrete by the factory, `consume_generate` calls the concrete's `emit` once with `EmitParams` and an `EmitPayload` holding the mint context, the transfer context, the identities, and the proof identity it holds, and returns the `EmitReturn` the call returned, a success's files in write order and a refusal's error, whole and unaltered
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the consumer is received only with its own forms: it implements `IGenerateConsumer<F>` for the forms its contexts are typed in, so the factory hands it only a concrete whose `Forms` are `F`
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the consumer names no concrete, curve, kind, path, count, version, or file; reads no file, environment variable, or clock; calls no factory; rebuilds no form, admits no context or identity, and pairs no identity with a setup scope; every value it emits with arrives in its constructor and every behavior of the emission is the concrete's

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   App module of the harness in the adapter role, beneath its `run` module: a class implementing a repo-owned trait, the `IGenerateConsumer<F>` that `harness-crypto/generate/factory` declares, holding the values it emits with and wrapping no outer-edge collaborator of its own, as `harness-crypto/benchmark` is the pairing family's consumer
    * `[✅]`   Owns `GenerateConsumer`, `GenerateConsumerConstructorParams`, `GenerateConsumerTryNewReturn`, and `GenerateConsumerReturn`

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/run/key_agreement_consumer` **The run's consumer of the envelope family's factory: `KeyAgreementConsumer`, an `IKeyAgreementConsumer<P>` generic over the pairing and the chain forms its contexts are typed in, constructed from the borrowed pairing, hash-to-scalar adapter, encoder, randomness source, and credential KEM, the typed mint and transfer contexts, the admitted identities, the admitted proof identity, the configured delivery proof selection, generate selection with its per-kind configuration, verifier concrete of the curve it composes, statement version, and iteration count, which on receiving the key agreement concrete the factory admitted constructs a `DeliveryProofConsumer` over it and calls `create_delivery_proof` with the held delivery proof selection, the algebra the received concrete declares, and the held statement version, and returns the files written and the measurement, or the first refusal whole; names no concrete, reads no configuration, and admits nothing; owns the consumer, its constructor params, its error return, and its return alias; adds the module to the `run` module**

    * `[✅]`   Functional: the consumer is constructed once from the pairing, the hash-to-scalar adapter, the encoder, and the randomness source, each borrowed, a mint context typed `DeliveryContext<F, MintPurpose>`, a transfer context typed `DeliveryContext<F, TransferPurpose>`, the `AdmittedIdentities` the configuration admitted, borrowed, the `AdmittedKeyAgreement` selection, the `DeliveryProofConcrete` selection, the `GenerateConcrete` selection and its `Vec<VectorKindConfiguration>`, the `VerifierConcrete` of the one curve the consumer composes, the delivery-statement version as a `u16`, and the iteration count as a `NonZeroU32`, each handed typed and admitted, and holds them for every call it makes
    * `[✅]`   Functional: handed a KEM concrete by the factory, `consume_kem` builds one `AdmittedKemIdentity` for the held proof identity and one for each held mapping identity, in the order held, by pairing the admitted identity's variant with the setup scope and the `KemIdentity` that variant names, constructs one `KeyAgreementConsumer` from the held adapters, the received concrete, clones of the held contexts, those identities, and the held configured values, and calls `create_key_agreement` once, over the held pairing, hash-to-scalar adapter, encoder, and randomness source, with the held key agreement selection's `concrete`, `identifier`, and `algebra`; a refusal of `create_key_agreement` returns, and a refusal the key agreement consumer returns as its output returns; when both succeed it returns the key agreement consumer's success, whole and unaltered

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/run/chain_forms_consumer` **The run's consumer of the chain family's forms function: `ChainFormsConsumer`, an `IChainFormsConsumer` constructed from the borrowed pairing, hash-to-scalar adapter, encoder, and randomness source, the borrowed `AdmittedStatements` and `AdmittedIdentities`, the admitted KEM, key agreement, and delivery proof selections, the generate selection with its per-kind configuration, the verifier concrete of the curve the consumer composes, the statement version, and the iteration count, which on receiving the forms the function constructed admits the mint and the transfer canonical contexts into those forms through `admit_contexts`, constructs a `KemConsumer` over the typed contexts, calls `create_kem` with the held KEM selection, and returns the files written and the measurement, or the first refusal whole; names no concrete, reads no configuration, and admits no selection or identity; owns the consumer, its constructor params, its error return, and its return alias; adds the module to the `run` module**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the harness composes every later family over whichever forms `create_chain_forms` constructs from the configured selection, and the forms' type is the type the typed delivery contexts, the KEM consumer, and every consumer beneath it are generic over, so the function hands the forms to a consumer rather than returning them; the run therefore holds a consumer that receives the forms, rebuilds each canonical context into them once through `admit_contexts`, builds the `KemConsumer` over the typed contexts, and calls the KEM family's factory, so that `harness-crypto/run/encoding_consumer` passes the consumer to `create_chain_forms` and no run module names a forms or KEM concrete, reads a configured value, or admits a selection or an identity (the dependency map's `harness-crypto/run/chain_forms_consumer` row, which, handed the forms concrete, admits the configured mint and transfer contexts with their purposes into typed contexts in those forms through `harness-crypto/run/admit_contexts`, returns a refusal unchanged, then calls `create_kem` with the configured KEM selection, its consumer `harness-crypto/run/kem_consumer`, carrying the typed contexts and the configured verifier concrete of the curve it composes, which carries that curve's deployment record, and the measurement settings to the consumer it constructs; its conflict flags on forms admission happening once in `harness-crypto/run/admit_contexts` and on the harness run alone calling the factories; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `harness-crypto/run/chain_forms_consumer`, admitting the mint and transfer contexts through `harness-crypto/run/admit_contexts` and carrying them)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the consumer is constructed once from the pairing, the hash-to-scalar adapter, the encoder, and the randomness source, each borrowed, the `AdmittedStatements` and the `AdmittedIdentities` the configuration admitted, each borrowed, the `AdmittedKem` and `AdmittedKeyAgreement` selections, the `DeliveryProofConcrete` selection, the `GenerateConcrete` selection and its `Vec<VectorKindConfiguration>`, the `VerifierConcrete` of the one curve the consumer composes, the delivery-statement version as a `u16`, and the iteration count as a `NonZeroU32`, each handed typed and admitted, and holds them for every call it makes
    * `[✅]`   Functional: handed the forms by the function, `consume_chain_forms` admits the held mint context into a `DeliveryContext<F, MintPurpose>` with the held mint purpose, then the held transfer context into a `DeliveryContext<F, TransferPurpose>` with the held transfer purpose, each through `admit_contexts`, constructs one `KemConsumer` from the held adapters, those contexts, the held identities, and the held configured values, and calls `create_kem` once, over the held pairing and hash-to-scalar adapter, with the held KEM selection's `concrete`, `identifier`, and `scope`; a refusal of either admission returns, a refusal of `create_kem` returns, and a refusal the KEM consumer returns as its output returns; when all succeed it returns the KEM consumer's success, whole and unaltered
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the consumer is received only with forms it admits into: it implements `IChainFormsConsumer` for every `F: IChainForms` the function hands it, and the typed contexts it constructs are in those forms
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the consumer names no concrete, curve, kind, form value, endpoint, address, path, count, or version; reads no file, environment variable, or clock; calls no other factory; rebuilds no form except through `admit_contexts`, admits no selection or identity, and does not read the declaration the forms carry; every value it calls with arrives in its constructor or from the forms it is handed, and every behavior of the emission and the measurement is the callees'

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/output` **The harness output: renders the run's outcome as one JSON document, a success as the benchmark's measurement of each configured concrete and, per curve, its label, the files written, its measurement, and its report document, a refusal as the refusal the run returned, and writes the text to each destination it is handed, standard output or a file at its path, in the order handed, returning the text or the first refused write with the destination it concerned; owns the render of the outcome and its deps, params, payload, success, error, return, and signature types**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: where the run reports is configured and never coded, and the run returns its outcome as values, so the harness holds one function that renders that outcome, success or refusal, as text and writes the text to the destinations the configuration admitted, so that the binary calls the configuration, the run, and the output in turn and names no stream, path, or format (the dependency map's `harness-crypto/output` row, its Dependencies edges on `harness-crypto/run` and `harness-crypto/config/admit_output`, and its conflict flag that the harness's output is configured, as its inputs are; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `harness-crypto/output`, writing the outcome, success or refusal, to each configured destination, standard output, a file, or both, and its exit, the run's outcome written to standard output, to a configured file, and to both, with the benchmark's measurements for every configured pairing concrete in a success; the technical requirements' Harness run output, a success carrying the benchmark's measurements per configured pairing concrete and, per curve, the files written, the measurements, and the report, or the first refusal, as text to each destination in the configured order, the process exiting with a success status only when the run succeeds and every destination is written; the product requirements' Resolved Positions on the harness output; `harness-crypto/run`'s `RunReturn`, `RunSuccessReturn`, `RunCurveOutcome`, and `RunErrorReturn`; `harness-crypto/config/admit_output`'s `OutputDestination`)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the outcome arrives handed as the `RunReturn` the run returned and the destinations arrive handed as the `OutputDestination`s the configuration admitted; the function computes, compares, and selects nothing, and renders each value as it was handed
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the text is one JSON document an automated reader parses with a JSON parser: a success is an object whose `outcome` is `"succeeded"`, whose `benchmarks` is an array holding one object per entry in the order held, and whose `curves` is an array holding, per entry in the order held, an object with the label, the files, the measurement, and the report document; a refusal is an object whose `outcome` is `"refused"` and whose `refusal` is the `Debug` text of the refusal the run returned, whole
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the text is rendered once and written, as the same text, to each destination in the order handed, a file at its path or standard output; a refused write is returned unchanged with the destination it concerned, before any later destination is written
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a refusal of the run is an outcome to write and returns `Ok` with the refusal's text; only a refused write returns `Err`
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function names no stream, path, file name, curve, concrete, count, or default; it compares no rendered value with any bound, creates no directory, and touches the filesystem and standard output only to write the text to the destinations it was handed

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/main` **The harness binary entry, the harness's process boundary: builds the `ConfigDeps` of the five admission and layering functions, calls `config` with `SHIPPED_DEFAULTS` and the process's command line, `run` with the configuration it returned, and `output` with the outcome `run` returned and the destinations `config` admitted; writes a configuration refusal or a refused write to standard error; exits with a success status only when the run succeeded and every destination was written; owns no type, holds no literal, and is the binary target of `apps/harness-crypto`**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the harness is configured and never coded, so the binary that a person or a script invokes reads its values from the command line and the shipped defaults, composes nothing itself, and reports through the destinations the configuration names, so that where a run reports, what it runs with, and whether it succeeded are all observable from outside the process (the dependency map's `harness-crypto/main` row and its Dependencies edges on `harness-crypto/config`, `harness-crypto/run`, and `harness-crypto/output`; its Sequencing statement that `contracts/evm/PairingLib`, `contracts/evm/DeliveryVerifier`, and `contracts/evm/deploy` follow `harness-crypto/main`; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `harness-crypto/main`, the binary entry calling the configuration, the run, and the output in turn and setting the exit status, and its exits, the harness run composing every family from the configuration's values with a file and a flag override each shown to replace a default and the run's outcome written to standard output, a configured file, and both; the technical requirements' Harness run output, the process exiting with a success status only when the run succeeds and every destination is written, and a configuration refusal, which precedes any admitted destination, written to standard error; the technical requirements' file tree entry for `main.rs`; the product requirements' Resolved Positions on the harness binary; the nodes of `harness-crypto/config`, `harness-crypto/run`, and `harness-crypto/output`, each of which leaves the call order, the standard error write of a configuration refusal, and the exit status to this node)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the binary calls `config` once with the process's command line and `SHIPPED_DEFAULTS`; a refusal is written to standard error as its `Debug` text, the process exits with a failure status, and neither `run` nor `output` is called
    * `[✅]`   Functional: on a configuration, the binary calls `run` once with the configuration and then calls `output` once with the outcome `run` returned, whether that outcome is a success or a refusal, and the destinations the configuration admitted, in the order the configuration holds them
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a refused write returned by `output` is written to standard error as its `Debug` text and the process exits with a failure status
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the process exits with a success status when the run's outcome is a success and `output` returned `Ok`, and with a failure status in every other case
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the binary names no stream but standard error, no path, file name, curve, concrete, count, endpoint, address, or default; every value it calls with is the process's command line, `SHIPPED_DEFAULTS`, or a callee's return, and it computes, compares, and selects nothing beyond whether each callee returned `Ok`

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   Binary entry of `apps/harness-crypto` and the harness's composition root over its three consumers of the process, in the app ring: the single `main` function of the crate's binary target, which Cargo discovers at `apps/harness-crypto/src/main.rs`, beside the library target whose barrel `apps/harness-crypto/src/lib.rs` already exports every item below
    * `[✅]`   The process entry takes the signature the language fixes for it, no deps, params, or payload, and returns `std::process::ExitCode`; the deps, params, and payload of each callee are built inside it from the callee's own declared values, the composition-root form `docs/agents/dependency-injection.md` states
    * `[✅]`   Declares no type, interface, mock, guard, or provides surface, because every type it uses is owned by the node of the callee that authors it; its outcome is observable only as an exit status, standard error, standard output, and files, which only a process boundary shows, so the public integration test element is the node's proof and no unit test file is authored
    * `[✅]`   Does not read a file, an environment variable other than the command line, or a clock; does not parse an argument, admit a value, call a family's factory, construct an adapter, render or write the outcome, or write a report, which `config`, `run`, and `output` do; does not retry, summarize, convert, or alter a callee's value or error
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit; the commit of the grouping sits on `contracts/evm/deploy`

  * `[✅]`   `module`
    * `[✅]`   Bounded context: the binary target of `apps/harness-crypto`, whose crate root is `apps/harness-crypto/src/main.rs`, depending on the library target of the same package through its public surface alone and declaring no module of its own
    * `[✅]`   Files: `apps/harness-crypto/src/main.interaction.spec.md`, `apps/harness-crypto/src/main.rs`, and `apps/harness-crypto/tests/main_integration_test.rs`; `apps/harness-crypto/Cargo.toml` and `apps/harness-crypto/src/lib.rs` are unchanged
